On the bench, each in a dropper bottle or a small jar: distilled water; rubbing alcohol (2-propanol); vinegar; vegetable oil; mineral oil; table salt (sodium chloride); sugar (sucrose).

Three parts:

1. **Liquid with liquid.** Two of the five liquids go into a test tube, more of one than of the other, and the tube is shaken and left to stand. One layer or two; and if two, which liquid is on top. The bench offers nine of the ten pairs, and every one of them can be tried; the part is done when six count.
2. **Solid in liquid.** A little salt or a little sugar goes into a liquid. It dissolves or it does not. The bench offers eight of the ten; the part is done when six of the eight count.
3. **The rule.** The learner sorts the five liquids into those like water and those like oil, from what the tubes showed, and answers two questions that the rule answers and the bench did not show. There are the two families and no third: the rubbing alcohol sorts with water, and the part's note says that its molecule has an oily part too.

A pair or a solid-and-liquid for which no source says what happens is not on the bench. Three are left off for that: the rubbing alcohol with the mineral oil, and salt and sugar in the rubbing alcohol. The lab's research record says why of each (`docs/research/mixing/README.md`), and the page says it where the tubes are chosen.

### 25.2 A tube

A tube is a short column of cards:

1. **The tube**, drawn and explored as in sections 8 and 17, with its layers marked, each once it has been looked at: one liquid, or two liquids one over the other, the thicker being the one there is more of; a solid at the bottom, or none. Until a tube has stood it is drawn as one cloudy liquid, whatever it holds, so that nothing is read from it early. Vegetable oil is pale yellow and the other four liquids have no colour, so the layers are told apart by how thick each is and by what is said when each is looked at, never by colour alone.
2. **What is seen**: choice boxes, opened by looking: one layer or two; which is on top; whether the solid is gone.

Guided checks each answer as it is given and has a hint in a closed disclosure. Challenge takes what is recorded and checks a tube when asked. A tube counts when what was recorded matches what it showed. No slip of technique spoils a run, and nothing is drawn by chance.

Each liquid and each solid links to its molecule in the explorer where the library holds it, so that the shape behind the rule can be looked at: water, the acetic acid of the vinegar, and the sucrose of the sugar. The library holds no 2-propanol and no one molecule for an oil or for salt.

### 25.3 Model and data

The engine is driven by its record, as section 18's is. Each pair holds whether the two mix, with a source. Which liquid is on top is stored nowhere: it follows from the two densities, each with a source and with the temperature its source prints it for, and with none where the source prints none, as for two of the five; the build refuses a pair whose two densities are too near to say which floats. Each solid-and-liquid holds whether the solid dissolves, with a source. Each liquid holds which family it belongs to for the rule, with a source.

Hazards by 5.2 for the rubbing alcohol, which burns; the kitchen things share the note of section 21 for foods on a bench.

### 25.4 Primer

Short topics, those to read first marked: mixing and not mixing, and the words for each (miscible, immiscible, soluble, insoluble); a molecule with two ends (polar) and one without (nonpolar); like dissolves like; which layer is on top, and density; why salt comes apart in water and not in oil; safety.

### 25.5 Acceptance

- Every tube the bench offers shows what its stored record gives and nothing else; which liquid is on top agrees with the stored densities.
- Every stored outcome and density has a source; nothing unsourced is on the bench.
- A part is done at its count; the sorting of part 3 is checked against the stored families.
- The stored record round-trips; no state a learner can reach leaves them unable to go on.
- Challenge shows nothing of what is expected before the learner's answers are recorded; the families of the liquids are not on the page before part 3 is done.
- The description in words of every tube is tested, and no answer depends on seeing a colour.

### 25.6 The page as built

Built on 2026-10-12, straight through, on the shell and the pieces the twelve labs before it made. The route is `#/lab/polarity-and-mixing`, loaded lazily; Home and the list of labs link it with its status, and the lab is the thirteenth of the activities. Where the lab as stored is other than this section first had it, 25.1 to 25.3 now say so and the research record says why; where the page is other than 25.2 or than the red cabbage pH lab's, which it is nearest to, the point on that here does. Its one deep review was held the same day, and the points here have the page as that review left it; the last says what it changed.

- **The shared tube, with two liquids.** The engine's shape of a tube has an optional second liquid lying on the first, and the shared drawing (`src/app/lab/Tube.tsx`) now draws it: a band of its own over the first, from its own surface down to the first's, with a bracket and a word of its own where the page gives it a word; a layer whose word is null has no bracket. Between the two bands is a plain line in the drawing's whole ink, 2 units heavy, where a surface's line is 1.5 units and half as dark. It is what tells two liquids of one colour apart, as two of the bench's pairs are; the card is white in both themes, so the line is the same in both. No other lab sets a second liquid: the markup of 91 benches of the five labs that draw this tube, 406 tubes, was written out before the change and after it and is the same byte for byte, and their page tests pass untouched.
- **Parts 1 and 2: a table to choose from.** The tubes of a part are chosen from a table and not from the paged list of the labs before: for part 1 a liquid down the side and a liquid along the top, so that each pair of the five has one place, the places under the diagonal being left empty; for part 2 the two solids down the side and the five liquids along the top. Each place is a button, 44 px tall or more, named by its tube, that says how the tube stands in words under a mark. A place the bench has no tube for has a dashed edge and the words "Not on this bench", stays focusable, and says why when it is pressed, in the record's own reason as the page words it. Under the table are how far the count has come and the links to the explorer. Until a tube is chosen the table stands alone, under what the lab and the part ask for. Once one is, where the part is 76rem wide the table is beside the tube, and elsewhere, as on an upright tablet and in a window 1280 px wide, it is behind a button, "Choose a tube to try", and closes again when a tube is chosen. There is no Previous and no Next.
- **A tube is one card.** Its name and how it stands; its picture with the steps beside it; under them what to do next and how the picture is looked at; the tube in words, in a disclosure that is closed; and what is seen, which stands under these where the card is narrower than 46rem and beside them where it is not, the picture and the steps keeping the width they have on a tablet. The steps are buttons that stay where they are and take a tick, and say to a screen reader that they are pressed: for a pair, the two choices of which liquid there is more of, of which a press fills the tube, twice as much of the one as of the other; for a solid, the one button that puts the liquid and a little of the solid in; then the shaking; then the standing. No step can be done out of turn, and a button that cannot be used yet says why when pressed. How the tube came out is a box under the card, with the check in Challenge, the way to set a tube up again where it does not count, and the tube's note once it counts. Under a tube that counts, once the part's count is reached, are the line that says the part is done and the part's notes; a tube tried after that has neither under it while it is under way.
- **The picture** is 320 units wide and 300 tall, drawn 280 px wide beside the steps. A tube filled as far as the bench fills one stands 204 units deep, so a pair's two layers are 136 and 68 units, in proportion to the two amounts, and the liquid a solid goes into is 136. Each layer is a part of the picture of its own: a pointer over it, focus on it or a tap outlines it and says, in a callout beside it, where it is, whether it is the thicker or the thinner, and its colour, in that order. A part's edge stops 4 units short of the line between two layers, so that no edge is drawn over the line. A layer's bracket and its word ("Top layer", "Bottom layer", "Liquid", "Solid") are drawn only once that part has been looked at, as the tube now stands, so that the picture's labels do not say how many layers there are before the look; the bands, the line and the parts' dashed edges are there from the start. Of a tube that was looked at before its card was made, as on a reload or on coming back from another tube, every bracket is drawn once the tube has been checked, and until then a part is looked at again. A tube a solid went into has its bottom as a part whether or not a solid is seen there, named "Bottom of the tube", so that a look finds out. The first look at a tube that has stood is stored and opens what is recorded; a look before that says the cloudy liquid and stores nothing. Nothing outside a closed disclosure says what a tube shows before a part is looked at, which a test holds for all seventeen tubes at every stage in both modes.
- **What is seen** is asked one thing at a time, each recorded by itself and folded to a line beside "Change": one layer or two; then, where two are recorded, which liquid is on top, under a line that says which liquid there is more of and that the thicker layer is that one; or whether the solid is gone. Guided has a hint under the question that is open, refuses an answer that is not right, and checks the tube by itself. Challenge takes any answer, marks nothing, and has "Check this tube".
- **Part 3** is one card of two sheets. Each of the five liquids has its two families as choices beside its name, and a choice is recorded as it is pressed: there is no button that records. Guided takes only a choice that is right, puts a tick by the name, and says of one it does not take that it is not right, under that liquid, the choices staying where they are. The two questions come once all five are sorted, beside the sorting where the part is 46rem wide and under it elsewhere. Challenge takes all seven with no mark and has "Check this part"; a part that is not all right shows a mark at each line and "Answer again", which clears all seven and sends focus to the part's heading. The part's notes are shown once it is done.
- **The bench, the links and the sources.** At the foot of every part and tube, as in the red cabbage pH lab: the sources, closed, and the bench, closed. The bench holds what stands on it, what each thing is, the links to the explorer, the rubbing alcohol's tile with its hazards behind a disclosure of its own, and the one note that everything else shares. The links to water, acetic acid and sucrose are also under each table, open, from the start, and open in a new tab. A tube's sources are listed once it counts, and a part's once it is done; before that the list holds nothing that names an outcome or a family, and says so.
- **The Record tab** lists how each part stands, every tube begun with which liquid there was more of, what was recorded and how it came out, with why where it does not count, and, once part 3 is open, how each liquid was sorted and what was answered, each with a mark only once it is judged.
- **Where the page is other than 25.2, and other than the red cabbage pH lab's.** A tube is one card of two sheets and a box, not a column of cards, so that it stands in one screen. The tubes are chosen from a table, since a pair is two things chosen from five and a list of nine names says that less plainly; and no tube is shown until one is chosen. Which liquid there is more of is chosen at the tube, as the step that fills it, and not where the pair is chosen, so that a tube set up again asks it again. The look is a press on a part of the picture and there is no button that looks. A solid's tube has a part at its bottom that the messages' own list of parts leaves out when no solid is there. Part 3's choices are recorded as they are pressed. A tube under way cannot be set up again from the page until it has been checked and does not count; which liquid there is more of cannot be changed in a tube that is filled.
- **What was measured,** in headless Chromium on the production build. At 768×1024 with the menu open, where a part is 497 px wide and 959 px of the window are under the bar that holds the tabs once it is stuck at the top (measured to that bar's own foot), from the top of the three buttons that choose a part to the foot of the tube's box: 644 px empty, filled, shaken and stood; 810 px with the first question open in Guided and 762 px in Challenge; 930 px and 862 px with the second question open; 874 px in Challenge with everything recorded and the check to ask for; 771 to 818 px once the tube counts, with its note; and 939 px at the sixth tube of a part, with the part's notes under it. The sources and the bench under that are 92 px more. The table alone is 646 px, and part 3 is 608 px open, 873 px with its questions, and 788 px done. At 1280×800, with 735 px under that bar, the same tube was 644, 810 and 930 px as first built, so that its questions ran past the window by up to 195 px; with what is seen beside the picture it is 567 px at every stage, 671 to 694 px once it counts, and 816 px at the sixth tube of a part, with the part's notes, and part 3 is 608 px open, 580 px with its questions and 581 px done, where it was 833 px with its questions. At 768×1024 a tube tried after its part is done is 810 and 930 px with its questions open, as the first six are, and up to 962 px once it counts. The line between two layers was read from the pixels of the page drawn at four times its size: it is rgb(63, 63, 70), and against the band above it and the band under it it is 8.9 to 1 where the liquid has no colour and 9.3 to 1 where it is pale yellow, the same in the light theme and the dark at both sizes. The least part of any picture is 67 by 56 px, the thinner layer under a thicker one; no button, choice, place, link or disclosure's hit area was under 44 px either way. The three step buttons stood at one place through every stage of each pair's tube in the two runs by the pointer, twelve tubes; in the run by touch a button read 1 px lower just after it was tapped and nothing else moved, which is taken to be the frame's pressed look and was not looked into; a place of the table stood where it was when the sentence for a place off the bench came up under it; and the other choice of a liquid stood where it was when Guided refused one. With each disclosure open in turn (the hint, the tube in words, the sources, the bench, its hazards and their sources, and all four at once), the hint of the second question, the table open over a tube, both tables and part 3 with its hint, 29 states over the two sizes, no point of any control's box or hit area answered for another control. With reduced motion asked for no element of the Lab tab had a transition or an animation over a millisecond.
- **What was run.** The whole lab from Start to the line that says it is complete, every look by a press on the picture and every answer by its choice: in Guided by the pointer at 768×1024 in the light theme and at 1280×800 in the dark, and in Challenge by emulated touch at 768×1024 in the dark theme, three runs with no error reported by the browser. In each, a place off the bench was pressed in both tables, a wrong number of layers was recorded first (Guided refused it; Challenge took it with no mark, and it was changed) and a wrong family was pressed for the vinegar. In Challenge, before the first tube's check, its card held no mark and no note and its list of sources was empty. A reload then opened the Lab tab on the complete lab.
- **What the builder did not check.** The keyboard alone was not used for a whole lab, and no contrast but the line's was measured: the review did both. The builder looked at every kind of tube in pictures taken at both sizes in both themes (not settled; one layer with no colour and one pale yellow; two layers with the oil on top and with it underneath; two with no colour, the thinner on top and the thicker on top; a solid just in, spread through, gone and left), at both tables, part 3, the Record tab and a page with every disclosure open; the rest of the hundred pictures were taken and not looked at.
- **The review, on 2026-10-12,** by an AI reviewer that had built none of the lab, by script in headless Chromium on the production build and by reading. It made three changes the owner had decided on: a layer's bracket and word drawn only once the layer has been looked at; the sentence at a filled tube, which told the learner to set the tube up again in a fresh one where the page offers none; and what is seen beside the picture where the card has the width. It put right what it found by using the page: the part's notes under a tube tried after the part was done, which made that tube 954 and 1,074 px with its questions open at 768×1024; part 3 past the window at 1280×800; focus left on the page itself by "Answer again"; what the last look said left in a screen reader's line after a step; a step done and a step not yet to be taken both announced only as unavailable; and a note that spoke of other tubes. It worked a whole lab in Guided by the keyboard alone, 1,693 presses, through all seventeen tubes and the three places off the bench, and Challenge by emulated touch through wrong answers, checks, fresh tubes and a change of mode; measured the contrast of every text (4.5 to 1 or more in the light theme and 6.2 in the dark, but for a step that cannot be taken yet, 3.7 and 4.9) and what is pressed, point by point, on eighteen states at both sizes in both themes (44 px or more each way but for the links of an open list of sources, 16 to 32 px tall as in every lab); worked which liquid is on top again by hand from the densities; and read every sentence a learner sees, the hazard note against its sheet. The lab's research record has the whole of it (`docs/research/mixing/README.md`), with what was looked at and left.
- **What nobody checked.** No person has worked the lab. No screen reader was used. One browser, headless Chromium on Linux, and no real device. No chemistry instructor has read any of it.

## 26. Endothermic and exothermic lab (added after v1)

The tenth of the labs taken from the owner's department's lab directions (section 17 says how those are used), and the third of three short ones from an introductory course. The directions set out two salts and timers; the procedure is the usual one, and each choice in it is the owner's to change. It is the calorimetry lab (section 16) made small: the same foam cup, logger and graph, with two salts and a short notebook that asks for no moles.

### 26.1 Set-up

Some changes give heat out and some take heat in. Dissolving is a change that can go either way: one salt warms the water it dissolves in and another cools it. The lab has the learner dissolve one of each, watch the temperature, and say which change is which.

On the bench: a foam cup with a lid, a stirrer and a temperature probe read by a logger every 15 seconds; distilled water; a balance; Epsom salt, which is magnesium sulfate with seven waters in its crystal; and calcium chloride, the dry (anhydrous) salt.

Three parts:

1. **Epsom salt in water.** A weighed portion, about 10 g, goes into 50.0 mL of water in the cup, and the temperature is followed until it has turned back.
2. **Calcium chloride in water.** The same, with about 4 g.
3. **Hot pack and cold pack.** Two questions the two runs answer: which salt would make a pack that warms, and which one that cools. It opens once a run of each salt counts. No source that was read says that Epsom salt is in a cold pack that is sold, so the questions ask only which of these two salts would suit, and the part's notes say what the packs that are sold hold.

Which salt comes first is the learner's to choose. The starting temperature and the masses are seeded.

### 26.2 A run

As section 16's run, with the cup, the graph that is explored, and the technique that makes a run count (the lid on and the cup stirred from the moment the salt goes in). The notebook is short: the starting temperature; the highest or the lowest temperature reached; the change in temperature, as a size; whether the water got warmer or colder; whether the dissolving gave heat out or took heat in; and the word for it, exothermic or endothermic. The first three are typed and the last three are chosen. The second is named for the highest or the lowest whatever the graph shows, since which of the two it was is the fourth entry; the change is taken as a size or with the sign it has. Each entry after the two temperatures follows from the learner's own entries above it. No heat in joules and no moles are asked: this is the lab before the one that asks them.

Guided warns of a lid off or a stirrer still, checks each entry as it is made and has a hint in a closed disclosure. Challenge warns of nothing and checks the notebook when asked. A run counts when its technique was right, its temperatures are the logger's and its entries follow; a run that does not count says why and can be made again with new numbers. No result is held to a tolerance, since this notebook works no figure out: a lid left off or a stirrer left still makes a run not count by this rule, whatever the cup then reads. Part 3's two answers are checked as a notebook's entries are: in Guided each as it is given, an answer that is not right being refused; in Challenge together, when asked, and answers that are not both right are emptied and given again. The lab is complete when both runs count and part 3's two questions are right.

### 26.3 Model and data

The calorimetry lab's cup and logger on a thin engine of this lab's own (`src/engine/hotcold/`). (As first specified: that, or the calorimetry lab's engine given this lab's record, whichever was the smaller change that left the calorimetry lab's behaviour and output untouched. That lab's engine is written about its three parts and its seven entries, so the thin engine was the smaller.) It steps the calorimetry lab's own recurrence, as that lab's part 2, with one of this lab's salts in the place of the ammonium nitrate, and reads what a curve shows and the technique that matters from that lab's own functions; two of them now take the three fields they read and not a whole run, and nothing else of that lab is touched. The heat of each run is the moles dissolved times a stored enthalpy of solution, from the NBS tables at about the strength on the bench, as section 16's is: +17.56 kJ/mol for Epsom salt and −77.689 kJ/mol for calcium chloride, each for one mole in 75 moles of water, the row nearest the bench's strength, with the figure at endless dilution stored beside it (+11.78 and −81.33) and used by no model. The cup warms or cools as its mass times the specific heat of water, and trades a little heat with the room. The cup's parameters are the calorimetry lab's, unchanged. The masses are this lab's: 9.60 to 10.40 g of Epsom salt, for a fall of 2.7 to 2.9 °C, and 3.80 to 4.20 g of calcium chloride, for a rise of 11.6 to 12.8 °C in a cup that reaches 37 °C at the most. The build checks from a cited solubility that each portion dissolves, and refuses masses that would change the cup by under 2 °C or take it past 40 °C.

The record names the hydrate: Epsom salt, the heptahydrate, takes heat in as it dissolves, and the dry magnesium sulfate gives heat out, about 85 kJ for each mole at the same strength by the same tables. That the same compound does both is said in the notes, with its source, because a bottle refilled with the wrong one turns this lab round.

Part 3's answers are stored nowhere: the salt for a pack that warms is the one whose stored enthalpy is negative.

Hazards by 5.2 for the two salts. Neither tile says which way its salt's temperature goes. What the default supplier's sheet and a government record say of calcium chloride and heat (the second tells of hot water that boiled violently when the salt was added) is recorded as read and not carried into the tile; the part's notes tell of it once the run counts, and the primer's safety card says, of no salt by name, that only water at room temperature is used.

### 26.4 Primer

Short topics, those to read first marked: temperature and heat; giving heat out and taking heat in, and the two words; where the heat goes when the water's temperature changes; reading the graph; hot packs and cold packs; safety. As built: six cards, about 650 words in all, four of them marked. The primer is not kept back in either mode, so it says of neither salt which way its temperature goes; the card on packs says how an instant pack works and names no salt.

### 26.5 Acceptance

- For seeds 1 to 100, a careful run of each salt counts: Epsom salt's cup cools and calcium chloride's warms, each by enough to read on the logger and by no more than a cup of water can stand.
- Each error of technique alone makes a run not count, and its cause is named; Guided warns first. (By the rule of 26.2 and not by a tolerance. Over the same seeds the reading is then 0.2 to 0.3 °C short for Epsom salt, and 0.9 to 1.2 °C short for calcium chloride.)
- An entry that does not follow from the learner's own readings is refused or marked.
- The same seed and actions give the same run; the stored record round-trips; no state a learner can reach leaves them unable to go on.
- Challenge shows nothing of what is expected before the entries are made: which salt warms and which cools is not on the page before its run.
- The calorimetry lab's tests pass untouched and its generated record is unchanged.

### 26.6 The page as built

Built on 2026-10-12, straight through, on the shell and the pieces of the labs before it, with nothing lifted into the shell. The route is `#/lab/endothermic-and-exothermic`, loaded lazily; Home, the list of labs and the command palette show it as "Endothermic and exothermic", the fourteenth lab. Where the page is other than 26.2 has it, or than the freezing point depression lab's page (23.7), which it is laid out as, the point below says so.

- **Lab tab: one part at a time,** with the shared paged list and how far each part has come in words beside a mark. A line over the list says that parts 1 and 2 can be done in either order. Part 3 says "Opens after parts 1 and 2" until both runs count.
- **A run is a column of cards,** each there when the engine has a use for it: the bench and the cup from the start; the graph once the logger is started; the notebook and "This run" once the salt is in.
- **The bench** is three lines (the cup, the 50.0 mL of water, the salt by its name and what it is, with what the balance reads) and the salt's tile: its name, its formula and its hazards, closed. Both salts are "a white solid". No molar mass is shown: the notebook asks for no moles.
- **The cup** is the shared foam cup with the weighing boat beside it, drawn the same for either salt, and four steps: the lid, the stirrer, the logger, and the salt. Nothing in the drawing or in the cup's words says what the temperature does.
- **The bench and the cup fold as the salt goes in. Other than the freezing point depression lab,** which folds when its solution is weighed and its notebook opens. Here the notebook opens on a press of "Run on", when the curve has turned back, and a fold at that press would move the button under the hand. Nothing more is done to the cup once the salt is in but to let time pass, which is done at the graph, so the two cards fold at that press, each under one line, with everything they held still in them. The window is scrolled by as much as the cup's card moved, and focus goes to its line. From then on nothing over "Run on" changes.
- **Run on is over the graph,** as in 16.7, with what one press does said beside it. **Added by the review:** a line under it, always there and empty until the curve has turned back, which then says "The curve has turned back: the notebook is open, under the graph." until the first entry is made. The notebook opens under the graph, and as it opened its card began 1,205 px down a window of 1,024 px at 768×1024, 1,119 px down one of 800 px at 1280×800 and 761 px down one of 768 px at 1366×768, with focus still on "Run on" and nothing said. The line stands in one box with the line for a press that is refused, so the card is no longer while both are empty, and 20 px longer while it is said.
- **The graph** is the shared logger graph on the calorimetry lab's axis, with one dashed line where the salt was added. No reading is printed beside it: a temperature is on the page only in a point's callout when the point is looked at, and in the graph described in words, which is behind a closed disclosure.
- **The notebook** is the shared worksheet with this lab's six entries: three typed, three as two choice boxes each. One entry waits at a time. In Guided each has a hint, closed, which says how the entry is found and gives no figure and no answer. **No calculator, other than the two labs before it:** the one thing worked out is the difference of two temperatures. **Added to the shared worksheet for this lab:** room over the hint of an entry that is chosen. Open, the hint's hit area reaches up from its line and lay over the foot of the "Enter" button above it; the builder measured the same on the calorimetry lab's choice entry, 6 px of that button, and left that lab and the freezing point depression lab as they were, since their pages were not this build's to change. The review measured both (16.7 and 23.7, last points) and both now ask for the room, so every page that draws the worksheet has it.
- **This run** says whether the notebook has been checked, and then that the run counts or that it does not and why; focus goes there when the notebook is checked. "Run this part again" is under it. Once a run of the part counts, and not before, a closed disclosure holds the record's notes on the part.
- **Part 3** is the shared worksheet again, with two entries that are choices of the two salts. Until both runs count, one sentence says what opens it. Answers that are checked and not both right have "Answer again", which empties both and puts focus on the part's heading. Once both are right the part's notes are there to open.
- **Sources** are at the foot of each part from the start. The tables the enthalpy is worked from, the atomic weights and the pages behind the notes are listed only once the run counts, since they stand beside sentences that say which way the heat went.
- **What Challenge is kept from before a run counts:** which way the salt's temperature goes, in any tile, note, hint or list of sources; the figure the cup runs on; and whether an entry follows, until the notebook is checked. Automated tests search the part's markup at every step of a careful run of each salt up to the check, outside the notebook's own choices and the graph in words, and after a check that does not count.
- **Record tab:** one table with every run of each salt, what was entered and how it came out, why a run did not count, and part 3's two answers, with whether each is right once the bench says so.
- **How long a run's page is,** measured at 768×1024 with the menu open, where the window has 959 px under the tab bar. The bench's card is 254 px and the cup's 328 px until the salt is in, and 90 px each after; the graph's is 584 px. So the cup and the graph, with the 12 px between them, are 924 px before the salt: inside one screen. The whole page is 1,127 px at Start (1,171 px in part 2, whose salt's name is longer), 1,722 px with the logger started, 1,613 px as the salt goes in, 1,875 px as the notebook opens (1,855 px as built; the line under "Run on" is the 20 px), 1,911 px with the second entry waiting, 2,109 px with the fourth entry waiting, 2,385 px with the sixth, and 1,853 px when the run is finished: two screens, and two and a half while the last entry waits. (The review measured the lengths at this size again, with the builder's script on the page as it left it: each is as given here.) At 1280×800, where 735 px are under the bar, the cup and the graph are 278 and 592 px, 882 px with the room between them: over one screen there.
- **Nothing moves under the hand.** "Run on" stood at one place in the window through every press of it, in sixteen runs of a salt at the two sizes, measured before each press and after the last.
- **What is pressed.** With each disclosure open in turn, on six states of the page at both sizes, every point of every control's box and of the hit area laid over a disclosure's line was asked which control answers there: none answered for another once the hint had its room, and the least room between a laid area and the next control was 4 px. No button, field or disclosure's area was under 44 px either way; the links of an open list of sources are the height of their text, as in every lab.
- **Not done by a person.** The lab was worked by script in headless Chromium by the AI coding assistant that built it, from Start to the line that says it is complete: in Guided and in Challenge, each at 768×1024 and at 1280×800 in both themes, Challenge at the smaller size by emulated touch, each temperature typed from a point's callout and each choice made from the two temperatures typed. `docs/for-institutions.md` says what was run and what was not. No person has run the lab, no screen reader was used, one browser was tried and no real device. No chemist or chemistry instructor has read the record, the notes or the primer.
- **The review of 2026-10-12,** by an AI reviewer that had built none of the lab, by script in headless Chromium on the production build and by reading. Its own among the points above: the line under "Run on" that says the notebook is open, and the hint's room in the two other labs that draw the worksheet. It also put right the primer's first card, which said that its two cards not marked to read first could wait until the bench sent the learner to them, where nothing on this bench does (they are now said to be for the notebook's fifth entry and for part 3), and Epsom salt's hazard note, which had the supplier's sheet call the salt an irritant that brings pain in the abdomen where the sheet lists the two as acute effects. It worked a whole lab in Guided by the keyboard alone in each order of the two salts, the choice boxes among what was pressed, and a whole lab in Challenge by emulated touch in each order, with the lid left off, the stirrer left still, a temperature a tenth off and each wrong choice on the way; measured the contrast of every text, and what answers at every point of every control; read every sentence; and worked the three enthalpies and a seeded run of each salt again by hand. No stored value or parameter was changed. What it left for the owner or a chemist, with what nobody checked, is the last section of `docs/research/hotcold/README.md`.
